**Input checked:** user-uploaded `Pasted text(20261118-153132).txt` (75,457 bytes, 554 lines), an engineering research and proposed drop-in implementation, **not** actual PR source or measured robot evidence. GitHub was accessible through the connected account on November 18; the report's claim that `hungngodev/desk-robot-remix` is inaccessible is not true of this authenticated inspection. Draft PR #17 remains the **only** implementation branch. Do not create another firmware skeleton or supplant current pins, code, ledgers, shopping list, or state machine.

| Report section / proposal | Decision against the actual November 18 PR #17 |
| --- | --- |
| XiaoZhi 2.6 / ESP-IDF | Keep the pinned real upstream SHA and existing ESP-IDF 6.1 Sense CI. Do not blindly upgrade an SDK, change partition layout, or transplant a different s3cam board to match a prose report. Verify upstream pin/SDK claims against selected target and exact source before updates. |
| Generic S3-CAM N16R8 / C3 coprocessor / INMP441 | **Reject hardware substitution.** This robot has XIAO ESP32S3 Sense onboard PDM microphone/camera and WROOM32 motor/sensor body, not a generic N16R8 camera module or new ESP32-C3. Published generic S3 pin numbers are not the selected board's electrical design. |
| GPIO19/20, PSRAM, boot pins and UART | Preserve canonical Sense UART TX2/RX1 to WROOM RX16/TX17 and current Sense PDM, OLED, amplifier and camera assignments. Do not move UART to GPIO43/44, wire an 8-bit OV2640 breakout, or use the report's C3 GPIO8/9 strap-sensitive I2C map. Physical J3, routing and revision must still be inspected. |
| 2x XSHUT ToF and fixed distances | **Reject replacement.** Eight generic VL53L0X are selected through eight isolated TCA9548A 0x70 lanes, all at 0x29, with 0x71 accessory isolation. The four downward and one front channels are local safety inputs; three additional directions are read-only. Never use the report's hardcoded 75/80/90 mm cliff limits as calibrated facts. Inspect exact GY-530 PCB and 3.3V pullups and physically measure surface, mounting geometry and stopping distance. |
| TCRT5000 and TTP223 | Not selected as movement authority/head touch substitutes. TCRT5000 may not be made a cliff safety source; existing head touch is AT42QT1010 with independent sensor path. |
| Cliff local 15 ms brake-and-reverse | **Reject automatic reverse and unconditional latency guarantee.** Existing WROOM local invalid/stale/cliff STOP+latch, independent hardware motor-cutoff proposal, operator rearm and Mac safety veto take priority. Driver currently uses LOW/LOW coast, not unqualified HIGH/HIGH active braking. Measured loaded stop time and CPU-halt cutoff still required. |
| Shared 5V loads, cell and charge | Accept power rail and brownout risk as valid. **Do not assume** a 2S battery and 3.3V converter are qualified or replace existing protected 1S candidate without electrical, charger, boost, mass, fit, dock and current evaluation. Both plans lack physical qualification. Exact battery, BMS, power-path, connector, temperature and dock remain HOLD. |
| MAX98357A, sample rates and AEC | Retain selected generic MAX98357A and current 32 kHz Sense output / 16 kHz PDM input with real PCM reference conversion, rather than force report's unrelated 16/16 INMP441 example. `sense_reference_codec.h` now compile-asserts both selected board sample rates to prevent silent reference loss. Physical double-talk, timing, SPL and acoustic delay remain unmeasured. |
| Continuous camera and proactive perception | Existing consented MJPEG acquisition, bounded frame selection, motion/illumination gate, safety-first source priorities, VAD preemption and stale result cancellation are the owners. Do not replace with on-chip JPEG->RGB565/ESP-DL pipeline until feasible on selected Sense with licensed model and measured CPU/audio interference. |
| WakeWordInvoke long context / MCP | Valid protocol caution. Keep short wake/detect tokens; use established authenticated read-only `self.pet.get_sensory_state` and current turn receipts for rich telemetry, not `SendWakeWordDetected` JSON text stuffing or an unauthenticated second bridge. |
| Natural dialog / tool scheduling | Existing Xiaozhi live Opus/MCP, Mac coordinator speech owner, completed same-turn tool receipts, quiet/cooldown policy and interruption are canonical. Gemini Live's SILENT/WHEN_IDLE/INTERRUPT is a **provider-specific optional** behavior, not a tested plug-in replacement and never physical motor authority. Verify exact live model support before integration. |
| Memory / persona / five robots | Reuse existing per-robot private SQLite, provenance, explicit peer consent and first-robot qualification. Do not install PowerMem, Cognee or a second long-term store by default; test actual recovery, removal, isolation, cost and latency before selecting a replacement. No autonomous persistent user-profile write by mere environmental inference. |
| Servo, face, open-source references | Retain approved head face/HSM and guarded existing MG90S gripper/lift paths. Do not force emotion or head movement on every turn, import GPL / noncommercial graphics code, or give direct model servo motion rights without licensing and operator safety review. |
| CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md / wiggum / extra services | **Do not overwrite** the existing AGENTS.md, duplicate engineering backends, install unnecessary plugins, change the single PR ownership, or invent a successful hardware build. Use small source/test edits, read-before-write, same-SHA CI, review comments, existing ledger, and explicit unresolved physical gates. |

**Actual source delta from this review:** `code/laptop/src/desk_robot/handling.py` now has a reusable `reset(preserve_shake_cooldown=True)` for its ten sensor fault, qualification and reboot pose invalidations, preventing the same handled shake from being re-announced simply because a transient status reset its 3 second host cooldown. Regression `code/tests/test_handling.py::test_qualification_revoke_keeps_shake_cooldown` covers loss of qualification, safe recalibration, suppression, expiry and full reset. Commits `5d5ecb9`, `af572dd`, cleanup `e1c002b`. Additional parametrized regression `c1a3d2c` proves the same shake cooldown survives malformed IMU samples, a genuine telemetry gap, WROOM reboot and implausible gravity. Source committed, **do not assert CI passes** until all four jobs finish on the exact combined current head.

**Additional audio protocol delta:** The pinned upstream `main/audio/audio_service.cc` `SetDecodeSampleRate` closes its live decoder before attempting to open an offered rate. A malformed 32 kHz decoder offer (confusing selected 32 kHz MAX98357A LRCLK with valid Opus input rate) destroys the still-good 24 kHz decoder and can leave subsequent proper replies silent. Sense overlay now refuses 32 kHz and other unsupported Opus rates **before** closing the decoder and allows recovery if a decoder pointer is absent on the same rate. Commits `eda23ad`, `aef1af4` add source and a regression requiring rejection before `esp_opus_dec_close`. These are not physical speaker or AEC verification; check exact-head firmware CI before claiming compilation.

**Outstanding blocking acceptance evidence:** actual XIAO synchronized playback/PCM capture and physical barge; non-fake playback ACK; loaded motion, motor cutoff and worst-case front/cliff optical timing; protected battery current, charging and dock contact; physical sensor PCB and electrical pullups; full 2D/3D printed fit and calibrated gripper; and supplier-specific priced first-robot purchase release. There are no physically qualified field measurements in the project ledger. No statement of final safe-to-buy hardware release follows from this source pass.

## November 17 source edge-case burn-down: vision, cliff telemetry, procurement

This checkpoint implements three missing acceptance details identified after rereading
the original long-form requirements and uploaded 61 KB architecture audit.
Only existing PR #17 and its canonical Mac + XIAO Sense + WROOM design were modified.
Source and regression commits, each applied with an expected-head lease:

1. `987855c8`: `VisualChangeGate` now rejects NaN, Infinity, nonnumeric or
   boolean frame change thresholds and confirmation counts, and bounds the
   positive integer confirmation count to 16. Regression:
   `code/tests/test_pet.py::test_visual_change_gate_rejects_invalid_confirmation_budget`
   and change fraction variants. This prevents accidental one-frame triggering
   or silent never-trigger behavior; actual motion optical calibration is
   still unmeasured.
2. `da53b76a`: `CliffGuard::diagnostic` now reports `valid=false` when a
   sample reaches its configured freshness deadline, while retaining
   `present`, raw distance and age for fault diagnosis. Previously the
   physical guard rejected stale values but the JSON `ranges.valid_mask`
   could still describe them as valid. The local STOP latch and rearm
   authority were not modified. Regression:
   `code/firmware/body/tests/cliff_tests.cpp::diagnostics_expire_validity_without_clearing_raw_or_latched_fault`
   covers the exact deadline, preserved raw sample, read-only behavior and
   uint32 timer rollover.
3. `f49219b4`: procurement blocks Adafruit branded **new** hardware when
   the evidence names a generic reseller but the SKU, brand, product name,
   product URL, resolved item or revision still identifies Adafruit.
   Previously only the vendor string was checked. The same guard applies to
   pet roles, shared room parts and selected linked decisions.
   Regression: `code/tests/test_procurement_release.py::test_adafruit_brand_hidden_behind_generic_reseller_is_still_blocked`.
   Already-owned boards and software library reuse are not prohibited.

**Verification status:** source changes and regression files are committed and
read back through GitHub. These tests have NOT been reported green on the

## PR comment cleanup and review dispositions

The PR conversation had 157 top-level comments at this audit. GitHub's
available connected comment API exposes edit and review-thread resolution
but not comment deletion. Nine materially misleading earlier notes have been edited in place
with SUPERSEDED/HISTORICAL banners while preserving their original reasoning:
* 6827922199: old premium VL53L4CD hardware direction; no longer selected
* 6826600053: old BQ24074 current assumption; corrected in 6829660823
* 6829510138: zero-step CI incident; later real jobs executed
* 6822424469: old AprilTag native detector semaphore release race; current
  detector holds its slot until its actual executor Future completes
* 6835889751: old runner and premium ToF purchase checkpoint
* 6836764600: obsolete VL53L4CD purchase qualification references
* 6859729090: outdated Pololu VL53L4CD breakout selection
* 6863547287: earlier zero-step CI no longer the latest runner state
* 6938790774: obsolete one-watt speaker selection and CI note

Inline source review threads F1 (CAP1188 register transaction), F2 (AMG8833
temperature band), R2 (USB rejection counters) and R1 (room callback contract)
are currently marked **resolved**. R1 is resolved as a *production callback
contract*: Runtime uses a call_soon_threadsafe one-pending-observation handoff
and the RoomSerialFeed docstring explicitly REQUIRES alternate callbacks
never to block. Python cannot forcibly unwind arbitrary third-party code that
hangs in a foreign callback; that theoretical limitation is retained, not
silently reported eliminated. The D15 DC motor heartbeat thread remains
**open for physical acceptance**. The full DC PlatformIO profile now defines
the heartbeat and main.cpp rejects unsafe full-sensor DC variants without
the macro, but the external CD74HC123 Q2 to DRV8833 nSLEEP CPU-halt cutoff
has not been measured on an actual board.

## Attached audit adjudication: do not replace the selected build

The latest external suggested plan includes useful references to Xiaozhi,
ESP-SR, Pipecat, LiveKit, Qwen, Frigate and proven peripheral libraries.
These are design references to consider through compatible adapters, not
authority to reinstall a second speech server, replace the active single
Xiaozhi speaker owner, or invent a second memory database. Review exact
upstream source and license before copying anything.

**Rejected as contradictory or unsafe until a separate reviewed design
change:** swapping the owned/ordered XIAO ESP32S3 Sense head plus existing
WROOM32 guarded body for a generic S3CAM + C3; reducing eight selected
inexpensive VL53L0X lanes (0x29 via TCA9548A 0x70) to only two XSHUT
renumbered sensors; accepting two repeated invalid desk-edge samples before
stopping instead of the existing local fail-closed guard; blindly assigning
unverified ESP32 GPIO maps; assuming every GY-530 breakout has 5 V-safe
ESP32 I2C pullups; or allowing voice-model MCP tools to send unsupervised
motor PWM. The second 0x71 TCA9548A is the separately owned accessory bus.
Mac remains the central intelligence, per-pet memory and fleet speech
scheduler. One physical complete robot must qualify before four more copies.

**Actual source guard added:** purchase generator commit `8bd8c2f` refuses
the architecture string or board identities silently changing to generic
S3CAM/C3 while preserving old abstract purchasing IDs. Regression commit
`c4745e7` covers the three substitution cases. Existing mux/sensor
purchase gates retain all eight cheap VL53L0X. This gate prevents a mistaken
sourcing approval; it does not make the selected architecture physically
qualified.

**Additional source correction:** `abd0562` documents the required
nonblocking RoomSerialFeed callback contract, consistent with the already
committed latest-only Runtime handoff. No new listener, USB port or worker
was created.

## Remaining active blockers, do not close as historical

* Full current-head Python/host pytest and native voice integration have
  not passed; a completed older run 44135807561 had 2,131 Python passes,
  90 failures and one error while C++ sanitizer and firmware compile jobs
  succeeded on that older SHA only. Inspect current run logs before
  updating this claim.
* Actual XIAO AEC double-talk, barge-in to speaker DMA/audio silence,
  native completed tool facts in live conversation, real playback ACK,
  context consent and five-pet acoustic arbitration require physical tests.
  Current native MiniCPM fallback narration is not proven same-KV duplex
  tool consumption.
* The 42 robot/dock primary roles, 7 shared room roles and 14 non-additive
  detail decisions are NOT 63 automatically additive purchases. Supplier
  PCB revision, 3.3 V pullups, eight-lane cliff optics, loaded stop geometry,
  DC watchdog, protected 1S pack, BQ charger and boost ratings, fitted
  gripper/chassis, contact-safe docking and thermal/audio load remain HOLD.
  The canonical full-feature plan records `physical_trial_count=0` and
  `prices_complete=false`. Never authorize the whole first-unit cart or
  five-unit fleet based on source-only tests.
* No new Adafruit purchases. Use established upstream and generic parts
  already integrated when the performance, reliability, cost and license
  evidence supports them. Avoid unsupported installs of Mem0, Cognee and
  Obsidian services as mandatory runtime dependencies.

The new source/test commits in this entry were accepted by GitHub but
have NOT been executed through full Python pytest or real hardware in this
audit. Continue small verified commits to the SAME PR, not a new PR.

## November 14 BQ24074 open hardware charger output boundary

Owner: Robot Open Source Research. Reconciled canonical PR #17 issue discussion (65 comments), submitted reviews (zero) and inline review threads (zero), and read current power sizing source and tests before this update. Research sources: TI BQ24074 product and datasheet https://www.ti.com/product/BQ24074 ; Adafruit Eagle board and CC BY SA attribution https://github.com/adafruit/Adafruit-BQ24074-PCB . The BQ24074 is a one cell linear charger with dynamic power path and programmable input limit up to 1.5 A. TI identifies its OUT as a 4.4 V system rail under appropriate adapter conditions, NOT the regulated 5 V bus required by the robot. With input removed, the power path can connect OUT to battery, so even a nominal 4.4 V figure cannot be used as a guaranteed 5 V supply. Charger current rating, system rail rating, battery supplement behavior, and downstream 5 V boost current must be independently qualified. Adafruit supplies Eagle schematic and board files, but its CC BY SA hardware license and revision must be reviewed before any commercial derivative. No code or PCB copied and no charger selected.

Status: RESEARCH ONLY, NOT PHYSICALLY QUALIFIED. Existing power preflight only evaluates current and efficiency; it does not certify the 4.4 V OUT rail, charge termination, thermistor wiring, contact faults, or simultaneous dock loads. Next electrical owner: obtain a specific carrier revision schematic, document actual OUT, battery, dock and 5 V boost nets, verify NTC and protected cell, determine charge and system current budgets independently, then instrument low cell and contact bounce, reverse polarity, charge termination, temperature and WROOM motor inhibit before enabling autonomous charge. Next QA owner: execute existing power regressions and record exact commit and exit status. Do not infer a passed test or approve charging from this note.

## November 14 TPS61088 switch rating versus 5 V output research

Owner: Robot Open Source Research. Reconciled PR #17 metadata at starting head `7ca7d4c077ac8110307f8df832b315a94916fd61`, all 65 issue comments, 65 combined discussion entries, zero submitted reviews, and zero inline threads. No physical qualification or charging approval.

Research: TI TPS61088 (https://www.ti.com/product/TPS61088) is an active 2.7 to 12 V input synchronous boost IC. Its advertised 10 A refers to internal switch current, NOT 10 A of guaranteed continuous 5 V output. The published 91% example is 3.3 V input, 9 V output, 3 A output, not the robot's low-cell 3 V to 5 V operating point. TI TPS61088EVM-677 is a reference evaluation board, not a qualified installed carrier or battery charger. TI BQ25798 (https://www.ti.com/product/BQ25798) provides NVDC charging and OTG regulation up to 3.32 A, also below the current 3.9 A margin-adjusted 5 V peak. No external code was copied, so no new third-party software license applies; manufacturer hardware designs require separate document and design reuse rights review before fabrication.

Status: source regression COMMITTED, test NOT RUN. Next QA owner: run `cd code && python -m pytest -q tests/test_power_preflight.py` on latest head, check CI runner actually executes, and retain evidence. Next electrical owner: evaluate a concrete boost carrier with inductor saturation, thermal limits, PCB copper and published 5 V continuous rating at minimum cell voltage, then measure simultaneous motor stall, servo, WiFi, camera and audio loads, protected cell limits, charge/load sharing and dock contact faults. ## November 14 BQ25672 charger versus OTG current research

Owner: Robot Open Source Research. Starting canonical PR #17 head `5f6a41211e37ceb3e8b911b2fac7441e1945ba04`. Texas Instruments BQ25672 documentation (https://www.ti.com/product/BQ25672) distinguishes up to 3 A battery charging from OTG output current regulation up to 2 A. ## November 12 continued camera and BMP280 source integration

The latest canonical PR #17 source is not a new framework or a second robot. The existing MultimodalOrchestrator's two native Pillow JPEG workers now own their semaphore slots until native completion, rather than only until a cancelled asyncio wrapper exits. One optional camera decode may run concurrently, reserving a second worker for current human speech or explicit visual queries. New test cases cover cancellation while the native worker is blocked, failed executor submission, and higher-priority human camera admission. An isolated standalone Python concurrency harness actually passed the slot lifetime, priority and recovery cases; **the full repository pytest and real camera hardware have not run**. No acoustic timing or camera exposure SLA was measured.

The BMP280 previously had compensated read-only values but no defensible source acquisition identity for proactive interactions. Its existing WROOM adapter now spaces accepted pressure acquisitions by at least 1250ms (the chosen normal-mode 1 second standby requires time for a new conversion), increments a nonzero completed sample counter on successful compensated reads and publishes the counter and age through the existing AUX UART/HTTP contract. The Mac validator accepts legacy absent optional keys but verifies supplied sequence/age strictly. The existing PetController now tracks an independent per-pet pressure baseline and requires two distinct above-threshold conversions, an explicitly configured and physically qualified `bmp280_pressure_change_pa`, fresh age and enabled `bmp280_interactions_qualified` before storing one firsthand `ambient_shift` event through the existing camera/memory/speaker owners. All five hardware profiles default to FALSE. No new driver dependency, power authorization, extra I2C master, invented weather prediction or physically validated pressure data.

Source and test edits committed to the canonical branch, **full repository firmware/host tests not yet executed**. Verify `code/tests/test_multimodal.py`, `code/tests/test_aux_telemetry.py`, `code/tests/test_pet.py`, `code/firmware/body/tests/guarded_body_tests.cpp`, compile WROOM full profile and instrument actual BMP280 unique sample timing. Cross-check the UART reply capacity after appended source fields. Verify hardware body load/pin safety before considering any motion or charging ready. Physical charger, fitted CAD, final terminal approach, actual speech interruption and one complete robot remain open.

An independently generated per-WROOM-boot `imu.boot_id` is set in `setup()` after motor pins are placed safe; this value is for sample provenance and is **not authentication or motor authority**. `HandlingGate` rejects duplicate `(boot_id, sample_ms)` reads, resets its baseline across restart/time rollback/uint32 wrap, and fails quiet on malformed/missing identifiers. Existing scales, thresholds, source provenance and user-controlled qualification are retained. Firmware, host, cross-controller and regression changes `6849e31`, `aa3ed90`, `5df0d0e`, `6f25cb4`, `3ffb57f`, `f76b4ca`, `91322e4`, `62a37ae` cover distinct conversions, replay, restart, rollover and WROOM JSON identity. ## November 11 followup: preserve human VAD through long speech and camera failures

The current canonical branch adds four small source/test/documentation iterations after the Reachy Mini initiative gate. The risk was in the real Mac media coordinator, not a missing AI framework:

* `CameraFrameHub.notify("human_voice")` previously guessed an end after seven seconds despite the server now emitting actual authenticated `speech_end`. It now keeps the human voice floor active until a real end or explicit disconnect/reset. This avoids reopening proactive speech during a long utterance. A missing end fails quiet rather than guessing silence.
* Failed XIAO camera capture previously called the full `hub.clear`, which also erased human voice state. New `discard_video` invalidates stale JPEGs and in-flight generations without clearing the independent live VAD evidence. Background speech-frame sampling continues at the existing approximate 1 FPS for up to 60 opportunities per utterance, not indefinitely on a missing end.
* `PetController.start` and `pause` now use the same video-only invalidation instead of clearing the Xiaozhi voice floor. Changing autonomous camera mode cannot tell the speech arbiter that a user stopped talking.
* Added offline regression tests in the existing `code/tests/test_multimodal.py` and `code/tests/test_pet.py` for speech longer than seven seconds, camera failure while speaking and autonomous pause during a live user turn. The implementation and tests are committed to PR #17 but **not confirmed passing**. The latest GitHub Actions jobs finish failed with no published steps, so no source-test diagnosis or acoustic acceptance claim is justified.

This is a small compatible reuse of the existing Xiaozhi VAD, LiveKit-inspired frame hub and PetController state owners, not a second media stack. The real XIAO microphone/speaker, live model speed, final printer assembly, grasp calibration, docking and protected charger remain unqualified.

## November 11 continuation: live monitor availability and distinct VAD event kinds

Second small reuse loop on the same PR #17 source owners, no new audio stack or branch:

* `VoiceEventListener` now reports authenticated voice transport readiness at bootstrap, valid ordered polling, reset, stream loss and ring-buffer gap. `Runtime` wires it to `PetController.voice_monitor_ready`, initially false in hardware mode; both the cheap initiative gate and the post-LLM speaker handoff refuse spontaneous speech when the monitor is offline. Live fleet audio is stopped on a global event transport fault. Test files `test_live_voice_events.py`, `test_pet.py` and `test_runtime_voice_priority.py` now cover loss, suppression and recovered connectivity.
* Exact event contract review revealed `normal` and `group` AEC interrupts have no guaranteed paired `speech_end`, unlike a debounced idle `speech_start`. `Runtime` now routes AEC to a bounded seven-second `voice_interrupted` priority window while explicit VAD holds until actual end. The same `CameraFrameHub`, unified voice floor and camera task are reused; new tests cover overlap.
* A healthy authenticated event feed is not proof of silence during an earlier missed VAD start. For complete recovery safety, the pinned server must expose a fresh, authenticated current speaker-state snapshot or an intentional rearm handshake; do not assert that a newly connected empty event poll proves nobody is talking. Real head AEC and listener timing remain unmeasured. CI still fails without recorded steps, so tests remain committed rather than verified running.

Detailed integration and uncertainty are in `docs/guides/xiaozhi-voice-event-integration.md` and `docs/guides/multimodal-voice-video-orchestration.md`.

## November 12 unified extra sensor implementation audit

**Single authoritative implementation PR:** [#17](https://github.com/hungngodev/desk-robot-remix/pull/17), head branch `impl/mac-hardware-safety-preflight-2026-11-10`. Historical touch, gesture, range and IMU work in closed PR #12, #15, #16 and #18 is superseded. No one should open or merge another sensor PR independently. Read [the comprehensive actual source versus physics sensor matrix](../hardware/full-feature-wiring-and-shopping.md#current-sensor-source-and-behavior-closure-matrix).

**New source changes in this pass, not physical verification:**

* MAX30102 paw optical contact now uses existing bounded SparkFun derived red/IR FIFO parser, a fresh `OpticalRaw.sample_seq` only on completed FIFO acquisitions, augmented WROOM `aux.pulse.seq`, Mac optional `paw_contact_ir_raw_threshold` with `paw_contact_interactions_qualified=false` by default, two genuinely new confirmation readings, release hysteresis and reset/replay handling. `PetController` stores confirmed `paw_contact` in individual firsthand memory and routes any unsolicited reply through the existing one owner. No heart rate or medical claims.
* SHT41 similarly uses **CRC valid new acquisition** `ClimateRaw.sample_seq` and bounded `aux.climate.seq/age_ms`; Mac optional calibrated temperature/humidity thresholds and `ambient_interactions_qualified=false`, two actual sensor conversions, private `ambient_shift` memories, no invented weather or body-wide room claims.
* The previously reserved PN532 lane `TCA9548A 0x71:5` now has a small Seeed BSD 3 sourced frame adapter `Pn532Frames.h`, checks command response checksum, SAM configuration, finite RF retries, ISO14443A UID and nonsecret cloneable FNV toy token. `AccessorySensors` has a bounded nonblocking state machine, one transaction per lower priority mux slot **after the required MCP23017 dock read**. Compact `aux.nfc` status reaches authenticated Mac `aux.py` and qualified private `nfc_tag` toy memories, with `nfc_interactions_qualified=false` by default. No card write, security/identity or motion privileges. This is still subject to real breakout voltage, ACK/ready timing, antenna and current trials. Some board variants may demand IRQ/reset wiring not reserved in the existing finalized connector map; do not claim an official final wiring harness yet.
* New tests in `code/tests/test_pet.py`, `test_aux_telemetry.py`, firmware `pn532_frames_tests.cpp`, `optional_i2c_io_tests.cpp`, `pulse_raw_tests.cpp`, `guarded_body_tests.cpp` cover replay, fresh optical and ambient counters, tag framing/checksums, removal/return, and bounded WROOM UART serialization. Source files in `code/firmware/body/CMakeLists.txt` include the portable NFC test. No CI has executed their steps yet; label **COMMITTED NOT EXECUTED**.
* All five actual sample hardware profiles in `code/config/fleet.hardware.example.toml` retain full features but require physical opt-in thresholds. The canonical machine readable `docs/hardware/full-feature-plan.json` now reflects actual source, not stale statements that PN532 or three side ToF drivers are missing.

**Post-review NFC integrity closure:** `PetController` now separately tracks `nfc.scan_seq` and `nfc.tag_events`. It ignores repeated HTTP copies even with changed event counts and refuses a purported number of new tag arrivals greater than completed new physical scan frames; resets become new baselines, never duplicate memories. In the same mux slot, WROOM now suppresses optional PN532 scanning and invalidates the optional NFC reading whenever the **required MCP23017 dock GPIO acquisition fails**. Existing C++ Wire emulator tests exercise this failure without extra mux contention; host test simulates forged tag event counter versus scan sequence. These changes are source additions with **NO PASSING CI PROOF**. Final physical PN532 I2C carrier readiness, antenna, power and IRQ/reset behavior remain unresolved.

**Explicitly not done:** physical charger protection and current sensing proof, terminal dual IR homing and isolation, final dimensionally fitted Fusion 360 printer-ready assembly, XIAO microphone/decoder interruption audio acceptance, true calibrated grasp pickup/placement, RGB/haptic actual device effect drivers, external room/plant station drivers, physical sensor time/clearance validation. ## November 12 second integration pass: proactive fleet and camera correctness

**Sole integration head:** PR #17, branch `impl/mac-hardware-safety-preflight-2026-11-10`. The new source/reuse truth is in [`docs/ATTRIBUTION.md`](../ATTRIBUTION.md#what-is-actually-reused-versus-locally-implemented-november-12-audit) and the exact data/control flow is in [the one multimodal guide](../guides/multimodal-voice-video-orchestration.md#november-12-source-truthful-natural-social-behavior-and-active-camera-triggers). Avoid creating a second agent/audio stack or reimplementing established Xiaozhi/PetController/Conversation abstractions.

**Verified source inspection findings and commits:**

1. New PN532, SHT41 and optical paw `SceneChange` kinds were absent from `multimodal.TRIGGERS` even though the pet controller called `hub.notify` before memory persistence. This is an actual exception path that prevented those features from working end to end with the camera hub. The trigger set now includes these and legacy generic touch; `test_multimodal.py` checks coverage. A permanent `test_integration_contracts.py` asserts all physical initiative kinds map to recognized sensor camera triggers, preventing the same drift.
2. A sensor interaction was previously prompted to the LLM as a *new visual change*. The unsolicited prompt now distinguishes physical sensor evidence from camera model assessments, explicitly refuses unseen claims, and suppresses responses aged past the configured observation window. `test_pet.py` includes source-specific prompt and expired-model regressions.
3. The hardware runtime could only mark a sensor event as `urgent`; the periodic pet camera loop did not run while any voice task was active. The existing `MultimodalOrchestrator` now owns a **bounded nonblocking** `sensor_event` camera task with per-robot 800 ms request coalescing, the same real head capture lock and existing two-worker CPU JPEG budget. Sensor tasks are cancelled when genuine human VAD or an explicit visual question gets priority. A rapid VAD `speech_end` followed immediately by `speech_start` replaces a previously cancelling camera sampler, so the next real utterance is not lost. This is source behavior with pending host/physical tests, NOT measured latency.
4. Previously, robot-to-robot group chat required an explicit request. `fleet_social_autonomy_enabled` and `fleet_social_min_interval_seconds` now support **optional, consented, short two-turn social reactions** to a small set of credible witnessed toy, gesture and object events. They reuse **`Conversation.run` and the one Xiaozhi device speech lease**, not another agent. Both source and peer must be enabled/unmuted, source and peer generations must remain unchanged, the healthy authenticated voice monitor must allow spontaneity, no live human may own the floor, and `fleet_text_sharing_enabled` must explicitly opt in or Settings refuses the config. The recipient hears a secondhand report, not an invented camera witness. The peer rotates for fairness and the cooldown limits repetition.
5. A mute/pause on **either** social participant now cancels the same shared task; concurrent human callbacks send just one cancellation to avoid aborting device speaker STOP cleanup. Tests cover no consent, two peers, secondhand memory, timeout, rapid generation change, back-to-back VAD and simultaneous preemption. None of the tests has yet been executed by CI.

**Source consistency contract:** `code/tests/test_integration_contracts.py` validates all eight actual VL53L4CD lanes, mux `0x70`/`0x71`, PN532/MCP23017 cohabitation on lane 5, unique WROOM and XIAO GPIO assignments, real owner file paths, source trigger coverage and explicit social consent. It does **not** prove safe wire thickness, I2C timing, charger protection, SD J3 isolation, fitted geometry or built hardware. In particular the two IR terminal dock receiver pins and qualified external power circuit still have no final signed wiring allocation.

**Evidence:** source committed; new Python/C++/firmware tests are `NOT RUN`. All four GitHub Actions jobs still report failure before any recorded step. [Runner pre-step diagnosis](CI_RUNNER_PRESTEP_TRIAGE_2026_11_11.md) remains the blocker. Do not mark the full robot, advanced natural voice, exact camera response times, autonomous dock, grasp or production wiring as qualified.

## November 12 additional source verification and natural five-pet speech cleanup

Continue to use ONLY the latest head of [draft PR #17](https://github.com/hungngodev/desk-robot-remix/pull/17), not historical commit hashes or a new PR. This pass reviewed actual current `Conversation`, `KokoroHeadSpeaker`, xinnan server bridge, `PetController`, `MultimodalOrchestrator`, API and original pinned GPIO. Its results are **committed source plus tests, not green execution or physical evidence**:

* **Slow private memory was blocking live voice.** Five independent SQLite persona memories are now read using concurrent `asyncio.to_thread`, under a five second deadline **inside** `Conversation.run`'s cleanup scope. Before this repair, an exception constructing the group histories could leave `active` stuck and a slow SQLite call could block the global audio event loop. `test_api_conversation.py` injects slow memory and database failures and checks cancellation and recovery.
* **Failed audio must flush before relinquishing the group lease.** On Ollama streaming, transport or task cancellation failure, the current robot's existing Xiaozhi `interrupt` is requested **inside** the live social `speaker.session`, not after its `__aexit__`. This preserves the priority of real decoder stop over group lease cleanup. Proactive single pet audio now also requests abort on partial transfer exceptions, not only cancellation. `test_streamed_ollama.py` checks both cases and the order of head flush versus lease teardown.
* **Tool error must not break Xiaozhi's speech route.** The original pinned xinnan `plugins_func.register` plugin in `desk_robot_tools.py` now returns bounded status-only no-retry error dictionaries on Mac transport exception, timeouts, HTTP failure, invalid JSON or unexpected shape, never a LAN traceback/secret string to the LLM. Its current per-device mapping and operator-only movement path are preserved. `test_xiaozhi_tool_resilience.py` injects mock failures and forged IDs; no new MCP server was installed.

It treats XIAO RGB GPIO9 as **reserved, not implemented**. This cannot verify solder joints, SD J3 isolation or battery current. `docs/hardware/wiring.md`, `docs/guides/interfaces.md` and `docs/guides/five-robots.md` were corrected where they still described already implemented source as planned or claimed a current compiler success that Actions never ran.
* **Group preemption cannot serialize behind one slow robot.** `Runtime._preempt_audio_floor` now launches all per-pet human interruption requests and the coordinated `Conversation.stop` concurrently, still gathers each result and reports a failed stop as an error. Previously one unresponsive pet cancellation delayed even starting the independent group speaker STOP. `test_runtime_voice_priority.py` injects a stalled robot and a group stop failure. Committed, **NOT RUN**; real device audio has no calibrated interruption latency.
* **Current registered Xiaozhi head owns the tools, not just a string ID.** `desk_robot_tools.py` now verifies `desk_robot_bridge.connections[conn.device_id] is conn` alongside the configured device to robot mapping and private tool token before invoking the Mac, including any operator approved pickup or stop. This rejects stale plugin calls from a disconnected socket after a replacement head logs in with the same device ID. `test_xiaozhi_tool_resilience.py` now provides a registered mock WebSocket, tests stale old socket ownership and continues testing Mac outage and forged device IDs. Source and tests committed, **NOT RUN**; no additional MCP server is created.

No full Seeed synchronous driver is linked into WROOM's critical cliff loop. Reviewed LiveKit voice-aware camera sampling and Pipecat Smart Turn as **references**, not a second imported audio stack.

## November 12 third integration pass: actual sensor video semantics and group VAD priority

**Canonical PR #17 only.** Recent direct implementation found that physical sensor-triggered camera captures previously filled only the bounded visual question buffer; `PetController._observe` did **not** consume those frames in its `VisualChangeGate`. The source now consumes each newly received `sensor` or `proactive` image once, in chronological order and per-robot identity, with age and generation checks. The existing cheap gate requires a real repeated localized change before Ollama is called. Background camera no longer independently re-fetches frames already supplied by the sensor event. The cheap filter now reuses `multimodal.change_fraction` so uniform exposure drift does not look like a new scene, analogous to Frigate's motion-first strategy.

**High-priority task preemption:** `MultimodalOrchestrator.sensor_event` coalesces events into a **maximum two-frame confirmation burst**, with a 450 ms inter-frame delay if no human speech or direct visual question intervenes. The real background camera request now belongs to a cancellable HTTP task, not the long-lived observer. Authenticated human Xiaozhi VAD on **any** head cancels optional sensor and proactive camera work across all five, reserving CPU and locks for the human turn. Explicit visual questions also preempt an obsolete background JPEG. The permanent observation loop survives cancellation and can resume. Physical sensors and local WROOM safety remain independent of camera cancellation.

**Further camera verification fixes:** A live `sensor_burst_pending(robot_id)` flag now prevents the existing proactive observer from requesting redundant JPEGs while the authenticated sensor burst is already sampling that same XIAO. A speculative Ollama scene compare is no longer allowed to occupy the one inference owner for 45 seconds after its underlying image has expired: `PetController._observe` computes remaining validity after its shared vision-lock wait, refuses expired work before the model call, and cancels an already running comparison on its monotonic observation deadline. A regression case `test_expired_vlm_request_is_actually_cancelled_before_more_camera_work` checks cancellation, no fabricated memory and observer survival. Source test execution still blocked by Actions runner provisioning.

**Natural response policy:** directly intentional source events (touch, petting, nose, optical paw, hand gesture and toy) now get a **12-second configurable maximum shared cooldown** rather than the ordinary 120-second unsolicited visual remark limit, while preserving the single speaker floor, event deduplication, human VAD, quiet mode and source freshness. This is still a policy, not evidence of human-quality acoustics. Automatic peer conversations remain separately consent-gated, two-turn and serialized.

**Latency measurement:** The existing `/status` information from `MultimodalOrchestrator.status` now exposes per-robot sensor trigger counts, burst/coalesced/deferred counts, optional snapshot preemptions, successful follow-up count and **Mac event-to-JPEG receipt duration**. No actual head exposure timestamp is certified. `test_pet.py`, `test_multimodal.py` and `test_integration_contracts.py` contain new source regression cases for proactive source integration, per-robot memory, global voice priority, direct question preemption, camera recovery after cancellation, lighting drift and touch responsiveness. **Tests are COMMITTED, NOT EXECUTED.** GitHub Actions still reports four jobs failed before starting any steps; this is not a passing source or device build.

**Hardware integration boundary:** An independent review of [Microchip MCP23017](https://www.microchip.com/en-us/product/mcp23017) GPIOB, [Espressif's pinned ESP-IDF 4.4 RMT RX](https://docs.espressif.com/projects/esp-idf/en/v4.4.3/esp32/api-reference/peripherals/rmt.html) and [OOMWOO dock-cycle](https://github.com/makerspet/oomwoo/blob/main/contributions/dock-cycle/README.md) confirms a *candidate* to relocate slow reed/PIR onto existing MCP23017 GPIOB and free WROOM GPIO36/39 for **two independent** direct IR demodulator RMT receivers. This has **not** been wired into firmware or physically qualified. Do not copy Arduino 3.x RMT calls into the pinned 2.0.17 firmware or claim terminal homing complete from an 84 ms I2C GPIO read. Charger, battery, CAD and real acoustic acceptance remain unresolved.

## November 12 terminal dock pin conflict source resolution, physical status unchanged

A current source review found WROOM GPIO36 and GPIO39 already held slow reed/PIR, leaving no two independent direct demodulated IR capture inputs. The actual Microchip MCP23017 register table BANK=0 confirms unused **IODIRB 0x01**, **GPPUB 0x0D** and **GPIOB 0x13**; Espressif confirms GPIO36/39 input only with no internal pull and an RTC/ADC/AMP glitch erratum. Rather than dropping original features or asking a 12ms mux poll to decode ~560us NEC edges, `AccessorySensors` now configures GPIOB as input, polls B0 reed (active LOW) and B1 PIR (active HIGH) only after a newly valid mandatory GPIOA read on the existing mux lane, and records separate validity and sequence. `main.cpp` consumes only fresh optional GPIOB status if the relevant opt-in build flags are enabled. GPIOA continues to fail closed for mandatory dock contacts and charging PGOOD; optional bus loss does not grant movement. WROOM GPIO36/39 now **reserved** for direct left/right terminal beacon receivers, **NOT fitted, decoded or allowed to dock**. The generated source checked harness and machine readable plan were updated together.

`code/firmware/body/tests/optional_i2c_io_tests.cpp` contains deterministic virtual GPIOB reads, transitions and loss while GPIOA still succeeds. `code/tests/test_assembly_harness.py` and `test_integration_contracts.py` track the renamed MCU pins and full lane map. An independent cross-file source audit in the GitHub connector confirmed the driver and plan invariants, but **no current ESP32 compile or hardware trial ran**; all four Actions jobs still fail before steps. Next hard work is a proven two-channel NEC burst capture decoder on the pinned ESP32 version, input filtering and actual baffle geometry, plus isolated contact/power hardware acceptance. The candidate pin reservation is NOT a safe, final docking schematic.

## November 12 hard critique of cross robot human speech and VLM priority

Independent live source inspection of `MultimodalOrchestrator._voice_activity` found a real five robot failure: a Xiaozhi human VAD start on robot A cancelled A's visual tool but **did not** invalidate a different robot B's in-flight or queued `look`. An expensive B VLM answer might complete after A's voice took the shared fleet floor. The correction now increments each robot's visual turn epoch and cancels all peer visual tool tasks on *any* authenticated human voice start, as well as existing optional sensor/proactive JPEG captures. `look` rejects new visual tool requests on B while another robot's human voice owns the fleet. The actively speaking robot A may still request its own authenticated visual question. The existing single camera/VLM owner, Xiaozhi audio priority and motor privilege boundaries remain intact.

Regression tests in `code/tests/test_multimodal.py` cover a blocked B VLM interrupted by A VAD, and a B request rejected during A VAD then succeeding after a true A speech end. Code and tests committed to existing draft PR #17. These tests are **NOT green or executed through CI**; four GitHub jobs historically fail before any step. No second voice server or extra PR added. This change does not prove actual microphone timing, five physical speakers or battery safety.

## November 12 later skeptical VAD and visual evidence review

Newly audited source failure: `MultimodalOrchestrator.look` previously reused an idle frame up to 600 ms old without checking whether that frame was captured **before the current user began the spoken visual question**. A natural conversation could therefore answer from a pre-question picture despite receiving authenticated real Xiaozhi VAD. `_voice_activity` now records each robot's actual monotonic spoken-turn start. `look` requests a new head JPEG when the recent frame predates this start, regardless of its nominal 600 ms freshness, while preserving preceding frames for temporal before/after questions. If the existing JPEG was captured after the turn began it is reused without another camera request. Two deterministic host regression cases in `test_multimodal.py` exercise both transitions. No camera exposure timestamp or physical microphone latency is invented.

A separate race occurs after JPEG transport ends but before `_prepare` worker finishes: initial VAD preemption cancelled in-flight network snapshots, but could still accept a late low-priority proactive/sensor JPEG whose network task had already finished. Both orchestrated `capture` and external `offer_proactive` now recheck fleet human priority immediately **after** CPU preprocessing before committing such a frame. Added thread-blocked VAD tests with independent peer voices. **All tests committed but not confirmed executing** because GitHub Actions still fails before recorded steps. Continue source hard critique and do not claim physical completion.

## The user's cross-chat constraints, all retained

1. **One complete robot, then four identical functional copies.** Five different personalities and removable costumes, not five different incomplete hardware architectures. Day zero design includes the entire desired core; physical bringup may still be staged for safe testing.
2. Do not substitute an easier starter robot, camera-only demo, passive chatbot, fake robot simulation, a different controller, or a new scratch-built chassis. Existing open-source designs, datasheets, guides, drivers and algorithms must be researched first, then adapted to the saved source models and controllers. Custom glue is justified only where an actual compatibility gap exists and must be disclosed.
3. The finished experience is proactive and multimodal: real camera observation without prompts, selected camera frames after changes or touch, multiple sensor triggers, vision grounded contextual speech, persistent private memory with honest firsthand/peer provenance, spontaneous activity, real interruption/double-talk while speaking, quiet and pause, reactive expressions, exploration and independent five-pet social sessions. Match the *interaction quality target* of Gemini Live / ChatGPT Live, not a claim of identical proprietary model support.
4. Small actions must actually connect end to end: signal/electricity → WROOM or XIAO driver → UART/Opus/HTTP → Mac policy and memory → physical result → failure/recovery evidence. Don't add a driver without its physical pins, model-specific calibration, telemetry, runtime consumer and test.
5. Require an actual key and wrapped-candy lift, force-aware grasp, place/release and safe retry/failure. The robot must be able to find and dock itself safely and recharge from verified rated electronics. Camera docking observations and PGOOD pins alone do not prove it.
6. Finished delivery should minimize the human's tasks to **buy, print, assemble and limited real calibration**. Coding, harness planning, schematics, BOM, parameterized printable source, flashing guidance and automated tests belong in repo. Measurements of unknown modules and physical experiments cannot be invented or performed by software.
7. User wants **exactly two purchase lists**: previously owned/reported/ordered and still to buy, with images where feasible. Preserve uncertain quantities (e.g. MG90S ~10) instead of treating a remembered count as inspected stock. Prefer widely used generic modules; avoid recommending new Adafruit-specific purchases. Respect the user's first prototype **$50 delivered cap** including money already spent; report conflicts honestly rather than downgrading core features silently.
8. Review each existing PR and all relevant original sources, including useful closed-PR code, but **commit only in canonical PR #17**. Avoid creating 17 more branches/PRs or resetting decisions because a later conversation was opened. Use small review, reuse, integration, test and verification loops and update this shared context with durable new decisions.
9. No external tool installation, Cognee/Mem0/Obsidian/Notion integration or account data sink is required to make progress. **GitHub is the source of truth**. The agent can use available read/search/build tools to improve confidence but must not condition work on local MCP tools or data stores unavailable in its session.
10. Never claim green CI, tested acoustics, qualified battery charging, printed mechanical fit, validated robot docking, calibrated grip or completed five-robot trials without recorded tool output and measured evidence.

## Critical electrical and mechanical truth

The currently selected full eight-channel sensor plan is **four floor cliff VL53L4CD, one front obstacle VL53L4CD, and left, right, rear VL53L4CD** on dedicated 0x70 mux lanes. A separate 0x71 mux supports expansion. Floor safety cannot wait for Mac, AI or WiFi; front, side, rear readings cannot silently grant permission to back off an edge. MPU6050 preferred, photographed BerryIMU spare. XIAO's PDM microphone uses its fixed GPIO41/42 and speaker is digital MAX98357A. Two 28BYJ48 motors/ULN2003 boards, two MG90S lift/grip servos, 0.96-inch SSD1306 and XIAO Sense camera form the existing mechanical/electronic core. No full robot is assembled. **Current `AGENTS.md` has been corrected so it no longer suggests old sensor families or absent firmware I/O.**

Original SMARS motor chassis/tracks, forklift, Bluino SMARS rack/pinion gripper, Sparky v0.1.0 or more rounded licensed head assets are the source geometries. The rounded style should keep the center face, OLED/camera view and the full key/candy gripper. Mechanical dimensions must come from actual purchased modules and Fusion assembly interference analysis. Keep STL source license terms, especially any noncommercial/share-alike material.

Battery architecture remains electrically unqualified, with a potential 1.5 A charger/load-sharing bottleneck; never connect an unprotected live dock or imply a charger status GPIO measures pack current. No safe automated last-centimeter docking just because AprilTag PnP is valid.

## Detailed acceptance gates to be demonstrated, not asserted

| Gate | Minimum proof | Status at this checkpoint |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Build source | Python targeted tests, body C++ host tests, WROOM firmware and Sense ESP-IDF build against pinned code | GitHub jobs reported failure without available execution steps/logs; investigate; do not mark passing |
| Ordinary speech interruption | At least 19/20 audible TTS stop reactions inside 500ms, at least 18/20 new intents preserved, 10 minutes robot-only speech without self-interrupt | Not physically run |
| Proactive vision | Real XIAO camera 1 FPS speech sampling, event selection, 10 objects under varied light, chronological two-frame changes, false-trigger controls and recorded first-answer latency | Software only |
| Five peers | Five distinct active authenticated devices, memories, personality and source identity, speech floor, no cross-robot images or token leaks, 30+ minutes stable interaction | Software tests / plans only |
| WROOM motion | Measured cliff and rear sweep coverage, fail-closed stops, touch/bump/IMU status, low battery and timeout behavior with realistic surfaces | No bench measurements |
| Gripper | Actual MG90S/FSR pocket dimensions, jaw force and object position data, 8/10 key and 8/10 wrapped candy pickups plus safe release | Code only, no tests on physical objects |
| Autonomous docking | Qualified camera intrinsics, AprilTag detection, protective bumper/funnel, motor-locked contact order, reproducible safe charging current and thermal/fault tests | Pose and statuses only |
| Complete CAD | Actual measured source geometry and part envelopes, printable licensed complete assembly + mating dock, support/clearance check, no collisions in Fusion | Fit gauges only |
| Power | Actual components and datasheets, 1S chemistry/protection, current peak, regulator thermal behavior, safe load sharing, isolated/fused dock and transport safety | No final verified choice |
| Procurement | Exactly two reported-owned/buy lists with correct per-pet quantities, user-approved common generic modules and realistic delivered price including past spend | Lists in repo; cost conflict unresolved |

## Original HTML docking requirement recovered on November 11

The original audited `ESP32 Deskpet Ideas.html` still has a close-range **OOMWOO IR beacon** concept (two TSOP38238 receivers, NEC modulation, normally dead charging contacts, maximum three attempts) in the active design references. The present branch includes AprilTag pose and guarded predock movement, **NOT** IR burst decoding, physically validated final alignment, a safe isolated contact supply, verified pack current or a safe terminal-docking retry state machine. [OOMWOO dock-cycle source](https://github.com/makerspet/oomwoo/blob/main/contributions/dock-cycle/README.md) and [Vishay TSOP38238 data](https://www.vishay.com/docs/82491/tsop382.pdf) are the reuse foundation. Do not blindly port the ROS2 stack or assign IR receive pins to the slow 12 ms MCP23017 poll; a compatible interrupt capable pin plan, real carrier breakout, license and power design are missing. Do not infer true charging from a ten-second battery voltage rise or BQ24074 CHG/PGOOD alone. See [the precise recovered gap](../hardware/charging-design-and-validation.md#reuse-original-oomwoo-dock-design-rather-than-inventing-a-final-approach).

Avoid unreviewed npm/Python wrappers and license-incompatible firmware transplants merely because they match a keyword. First verify pinned API shapes, electrical voltage, source license and integration target.

## November 11 live five-pet voice and dock interlock loops

**Voice floor source and native model reuse:** The existing pinned Xiaozhi server now derives a short global human-speaking lease only from a *currently registered physical head* that passes genuine server VAD. Repeated VAD-positive frames refresh the lease, two quiet frames end it with a short hangover, and disconnect removes the source. The native MiniCPM model reply is blocked before its TTS START and again before every physical 60 ms Opus frame when any human voice is active in the fleet. It invalidates model epochs, requests the existing immediate XIAO speaker flush, schedules remote model cancel, and frees the peer speech owner without an invented hardware playback ACK. No new ASR, microphone or media server. Tests in `test_server_bridge.py` and `test_native_omni_xiaozhi.py` cover spoofed identity, ongoing speech, late reply start, racing VAD, already playing Opus, disconnect and ownership cleanup. These tests have **not** passed CI at this checkpoint; native acoustic quality is still unmeasured.

**Installed dock status fail closed:** WROOM `BodyControl` uses the already selected MCP23017 and its real `dock.sample_seq` plus `dock.at_ms`. Once present, missing/stale/invalid GPIO data for 500 ms locally stops motion and prevents a new arm. Either single seat contact or PGOOD now triggers the *physical* dock lockout instead of requiring both switches and power. An I2C sample loss stops motors **without intentionally dropping MG90S holding PWM**; newly observed complete removal only clears the latch, never automatically resumes driving. `guarded_body_tests.cpp` adds the dry-contact, failure, stale and rearm sequence; bench tests are still essential. The charger remains unselected/untested and `charging_verified` stays false. See [dock charging design](../hardware/charging-design-and-validation.md).

Both changes were made **in the existing owners** and keep the accepted entire five-pet product. Other parallel chats must fetch latest PR #17 head rather than editing stale local copies of the bridge, native adapter or WROOM body.

## How to coordinate the parallel chats without losing anything

All parallel chats start from this file and `AGENTS.md`, the newest PR head, source map, product definition, and the exact changed files. Do **not** assume another chat's state from a vague summary. Each reviewer should choose a nonoverlapping part (e.g. body sensor safety, Sense/native voice, Mac multimodal, charging, grasp, Fusion/CAD), inspect current code and tests, make a narrow source change on the canonical branch, link the commit, and update this file with a dated findings section only if a durable decision or verified status actually changed.

For repeated small loops use a ledger: `requirement → latest accepted source → actual implementation → upstream reused and license → electrical/transport link → tests run → physical status → PR commit → next smallest gap`. Do not fabricate loop counts, passing green status or physical hardware checks, and never duplicate a component another reviewer already finished. An agent that cannot run tests must report `NOT RUN`, not `PASS`.

On November 11, the existing MPU6050 raw WROOM status was connected to Mac `handling_tilt` and `handling_shake` event edges, private witnessed memory, spontaneous speech arbitration and the existing selective camera hub. Config opt-in remains false and physical upright/gyro calibration is unverified; see [IMU handling](../guides/imu-handling.md). The sample identity, body wheel-state gate, hysteresis and source failures are covered by new mock test files, but no new board trial was run.

The immediate order of concern is **actual test execution and CI startup**, native audio protocol and model/head validity, power and docking electrical design, source geometries and printed fit, then measured grasp/sensor acceptance. Documentation and simulation assist but are not substitutes. After each pass choose the next smallest concrete gap; never claim the sprint is complete based solely on the number of commits or PR lines.

**End state**: a full robot with tested battery charging and actual reusable CAD/wiring/software that the builder can buy, print, assemble and minimally calibrate. Until evidence exists, PR #17 remains draft.

## November 12 ToF mux channel attestation and upstream IMU isolation

**Implemented only in canonical draft PR #17:** `9b209126` updates `TofBus::select` and `quiesce_optional_mux` to read back the TI TCA9548A control byte after a STOP and verify exactly the intended single critical lane or zero optional lanes. The existing `CliffGuard::bus_fault` path now covers acknowledged but stuck/mismatched mux selectors before any floor or front distance read. `9b2142c4` also requires verifiable critical mux disconnection before the existing upstream MPU6050/BerryIMU WHO_AM_I probe, rather than ignoring failed disconnection. The write path always requests STOP even if the byte write fails. The local WROOM remains sole motor authority; no AI movement permit or extra I2C bus was added.

**Existing upstream semantics, not copied driver:** TI TCA9548A SCPS207H section 7.5.4 and Figures 7-5/7-6 explicitly specify a readable 8-bit control register; each selected bit maps to one lane, and the STOP completes channel activation. Primary source: https://www.ti.com/lit/ds/symlink/tca9548a.pdf . The existing pinned Pololu/ST VL53L4CD adapter remains unchanged for sensor init, and all eight physical roles retain their prior mapping.

**Regression source committed:** `0eadecdd` extends the existing host Wire fake with selected-lane readback and fault injection; `7576136e` adds a host-only vendor init stub; `7ba921dd` and `71382663` add harmless fake Arduino definitions; `556ff243` and `658437a9` add fake mux tests for valid five critical samples, stuck critical mux, write NACK, read NACK, wrong/multiple lane readback, stuck optional mux and forbidden upstream IMU probing. `23e23abd` registers `tof_mux_tests` in the existing CMake/CTest sanitizer suite. `205cf9af` records fake attempted IMU reads. These tests have not been executed from the full repository checkout.

**Actual isolated evidence:** a separately compiled C++11 readback mirror passed eight lane controls and negative stuck, mismatched, read NACK and write NACK cases using g++ `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Werror -fsanitize=address,undefined`. This does **not** establish that `tof_mux_tests`, the source firmware, or the actual TCA9548A hardware compiled or passed. At the checked head `71382663`, GitHub Actions run 43285622699 failed before steps, as did prior four-job runs, so no repository pass evidence is available. Full execution: `python3 code/scripts/verify_system.py --suite body --report verification-result.json` or `cmake -S code/firmware/body -B /tmp/robot-body -DBODY_SANITIZERS=ON && cmake --build /tmp/robot-body && ctest --test-dir /tmp/robot-body --output-on-failure`. Verify real TCA current-select register, bus timeout and all cliff sample ages **on the loaded WROOM** before motion acceptance. Readback proves the controller register, not physically correct sensor placement or reliable bus wiring; retain physical eight-lane calibration and safety fit tests.

## November 12 live voice callback exception and fleet abort retry

**One canonical implementation:** Source commit `5e1e515` hardens `code/laptop/src/desk_robot/live_voice_events.py` without introducing a second audio stack. Any exception from the authenticated VAD event poll, including a failed user interrupt callback, now marks the live monitor unavailable and attempts the existing shared fleet audio preemption. A failed preemption is **retried on the next outage tick**, rather than marked delivered; individual fallback mode attempts each robot even if an earlier one is unreachable. ### November 12 research architect: shared fleet audio abort timeout

At PR #17 source commits `a37c23a` and `12f1426`, the existing `VoiceEventListener._preempt_all` now bounds the **shared** `on_global_interrupt` callback to 500 ms using standard-library `asyncio.wait`, matching the existing bounded per-head fallback. Previously only the fallback had a deadline; a hung shared audio owner could stall the voice event monitor indefinitely. On timeout the task is cancelled and a retryable `TimeoutError` is raised; no physical PCM/DMA flush is asserted. Added deterministic coroutine regression for a stalled shared owner and a second retry. The existing shared speech owner, all five identities, real Xiaozhi VAD, and WROOM motion authority are unchanged. Python `asyncio.wait` behavior: https://docs.python.org/3.12/library/asyncio-task.html#asyncio.wait . This uses the existing Python standard library rather than a new licensed dependency.

### November 12 legacy per head outage interruption ownership

Commits `cc1977c0` and `9d4d7936` extend bounded, cancellation aware interruption ownership to the legacy per robot fallback of `VoiceEventListener._preempt_all`. Without a shared fleet owner, a stuck head callback can suppress `CancelledError` and remain physically in flight. Every new outage tick formerly launched a duplicate callback to that head. The listener now retains one task per robot until it exits, while other responsive pets still receive an interruption request. The deterministic source regression simulates an unresponsive robot 01, two failure ticks and responsive robot 02, then recovery and later retry. These commits do not provide hardware PCM flush ACK or override the preferred single shared speech floor.

### November 13 research: terminal IR 64 item hardware receive ceiling

Research architect checked the current `TerminalIr.cpp` against official ESP-IDF 4.4.7 RMT documentation. ### November 14 research: BQ25895 NVDC SYS is not a regulated 5 V rail

Owner: Robot Open Source Research. PR #17 head at source read `d0b02321f8324ec654215aceea77704a24fdee6f`; 65 issue comments, 65 merged discussion entries, no submitted reviews and no inline review threads. Current source `code/scripts/power_preflight.py` and `code/tests/test_power_preflight.py` already reject the BQ25895's 3.1 A OTG output for the 3.9 A margin-adjusted robot peak; do not add a duplicate regression. Additional manufacturer datasheet review: https://www.ti.com/product/BQ25895 explains that its NVDC SYS pin tracks a battery-domain rail (minimum SYS voltage set by SYS_MIN), not a constant 5 V supply. Its 5 V OTG boost output is a separate mode and cannot be presumed to operate simultaneously with charging. Independent open-source hardware review: https://github.com/brickbots/PiFinder/blob/release/docs/bq25895_design_notes.md distinguishes VBUS, PMID, SYS and BAT and notes I2C/watchdog defaults. This external design is research precedent, not transplanted hardware or a license grant. A charger SYS rail must never be connected directly as the required regulated 5 V rail without a separately selected, measured regulator. Verify charger mode transitions, load sharing, reverse current, hot-plug bounce, dock contact polarity, inrush, protection, charger temperature and WROOM motor lockout with the actual carrier PCB. Status: research only, NO code changes, NO bench test, NO charging qualification. Followup owner: hardware integration. Select the exact charger carrier and independent 5 V supply topology, obtain its schematic/PCB and license, calculate combined dock and robot load, then write an executable topology validation/qualification test against actual selected components rather than speculative mock current ratings. ### November 14 research: MCP73871 power path is not a full robot 5 V supply

Owner: Robot Open Source Research. Reconciled PR #17 at head `e894c99f9adcd31b491fb1d5580d83698b401630`, all 65 issue comments and 65 merged discussion entries, with zero submitted reviews and zero inline review threads. Current `code/scripts/power_preflight.py` already requires a separately rated 5 V boost output, peak and sustained input ratings, and protected cell discharge; do not duplicate an existing electrical test. Manufacturer reference: https://www.microchip.com/en-us/product/mcp73871 . MCP73871 is an in-production 1S linear charger with system load sharing, input source priority, temperature monitoring, charge safety timers and reverse discharge protection. However its adapter mode limits combined system load plus charge input to 1.8 A, with charge current up to 1 A. USB selection is lower. OUT is a power path node, not a guaranteed regulated 5 V high-current robot rail. The 3.9 A margin-adjusted 5 V robot peak cannot be inferred to be supplied from this chip or its breakout; separate boost conversion, measured input current, dock load management and WROOM motion lockout remain mandatory. Compared with previously reviewed BQ24075 (up to 1.5 A input), BQ25895 (3.1 A OTG), BQ25798 (3.32 A OTG) and TPS61088 (10 A switch current not output rating). SparkFun LiPo Charger Plus https://github.com/sparkfun/LiPo_Charger_Plus is NOT an MCP73871 carrier: its source specifies MCP73833 and Eagle board files; do not confuse these chips or reuse that PCB as a power path design. Licensing for any eventual carrier must be checked at exact revision before copying CAD. Status: manufacturer and source comparison only, no new source, no executed tests or bench measurements. Next hardware integration owner: identify an actual documented, appropriately rated charger and regulated 5 V boost carrier, inspect schematic and licenses, measure docked simultaneous power and transient faults, then implement topology-specific source acceptance checks. Keep charging and assembly blocked and PR draft.

Existing 3.9 A 5 V peak and 7.647 A low-cell input calculations are for the mobile robot with motors and servos, NOT automatically the simultaneous docked charger load. Earlier notes saying a 1.5 A charger is rejected simply because it cannot supply 3.9 A at 5 V are too broad. A lower-current charger may be viable when WROOM physically locks out drive/gripper on dock and measured docked electronics plus charging current stay within the charger, connector and adapter ratings. This is NOT evidence that the BQ24074 is qualified: its OUT rail is not a guaranteed 5 V regulated bus; its DPPM reduces charge current to support system load, and battery supplement can occur if input is insufficient, which may cause unwanted battery discharge while docked. TI primary source: https://www.ti.com/product/BQ24074 (2021 Rev N datasheet, DPPM and supplement); Adafruit Eagle PCB source and CC BY-SA attribution: https://github.com/adafruit/Adafruit-BQ24074-PCB ; Adafruit guide revision https://learn.adafruit.com/adafruit-bq24074-universal-usb-dc-solar-charger-breakout/downloads (edited February 2026). No PCB or source copied. Status: RESEARCH CORRECTION, NO CODE CHANGE, NO EXECUTED TESTS, NO PHYSICAL QUALIFICATION. Next hardware owner action: measure docked 5 V electronics current separately from undocked simultaneous motion peak, verify motors and gripper are electrically inhibited before contact, choose a protected 1S pack and charger carrier, measure adapter input, charge current, 5 V converter current, dock contact bounce and reversal, battery supplement and thermal response under unplug/replug, empty battery and head reboot. Add topology-specific tests only after exact schematic and board revisions are selected. Keep PR draft.

### November 14 research: dock contact reverse polarity references and licensing gate

Owner: Robot Open Source Research. PR #17 starting head c8c65fe3fa413ef134f30484fc48e1c3212b639f (1990 commits). No tests executed or physical qualification claimed in this research pass. Keep PR draft.

### November 14 research: reverse input protection is not safe autonomous charging

Owner: Robot Open Source Research. Starting PR #17 head `4f0ac4657bb0eacc2c26e3158746511fa5dbdb9b` (1996 commits). Retrieved all 65 issue comments and 65 merged discussion entries, plus zero submitted reviews and zero review threads. The latest native audio and privacy issues are already addressed by source changes described in the PR discussion; existing WROOM dock interlock and dock detector regression were not duplicated. Source reviewed: TI BQ24074 Rev N manufacturer specifications https://www.ti.com/product/BQ24074/part-details/BQ24074RGTR ; Pololu motor driver ideal diode and reverse voltage discussion https://www.pololu.com/product/3752 ; MUREX Robotics open power board architecture https://docs.murexrobotics.com/elec/boards/power . The latter documents LTC4352 plus back-to-back MOSFET input protection, inrush and fault containment for a higher voltage ROV board, not a qualified 1S lithium pogo dock. Pololu explicitly warns that an ideal diode on one supply path does not prevent backfeeding through another connected supply path. Neither reference may be copied without checking exact source revision, license, topology, and component ratings.

Decision: keep these as SOURCE_REVIEWED design patterns only, not a selected charging PCB. For our 1S dock, separately trace all possible return/backfeed paths through charger IN, system 5 V boost, USB programming cable, WROOM motor rail and exposed contact ground. A reverse-polarity protected input alone cannot prove safe pogo contact sequencing, intermittent contact, fault energy, or charging. WROOM independent motor inhibit remains mandatory before contact; PGOOD and CHG GPIO states cannot replace battery current measurements. Next hardware integration owner: pick actual protected pack, charger, regulated converter, keyed/contact geometry, fuse and input protection components with schematic and exact licenses; then document worst-case power path and measure reverse/backfeed, contact bounce, inrush, temperature, cell current, and default-off motor behavior. Status PENDING hardware topology and BENCH_VERIFIED evidence. No source code or tests executed in this research pass; no physical qualification.

### November 14 research: eFuse reverse blocking requires external FET and correct input rail

Owner: Robot Open Source Research. Reconciled live PR #17 head 724511d5348ed00b1154a5478327cb652d3ef2ac (2000 commits), all 65 top level issue comments and 65 merged timeline entries, zero submitted reviews and zero review threads. Reviewed current power regression, which already separates docked and mobile current but does not qualify actual dock topology. No duplicate regression added.

Manufacturer source: TI TPS2663 Rev G (June 20 2024), https://www.ti.com/product/TPS2663 . This active 4.5 to 60 V input eFuse provides adjustable 0.6 to 6 A current limit, soft start, fault and power good monitoring, and support for reverse input polarity and reverse current blocking WITH an external N channel FET. The bare IC does not establish a complete bidirectional isolation circuit. Candidate location would be the adapter voltage dock input upstream of a selected 1S charger, NOT the raw 1S battery rail which can be below its 4.5 V startup minimum. TI datasheet reference design and exact external FET configuration must be inspected before any schematic reuse. Manufacturer datasheets are reference information, not an open source CAD license.

Counterexample: Pololu Reverse Voltage Protector 4 to 75 V 8 A https://www.pololu.com/product/5358/specs explicitly states reverse current blocking NO despite reverse polarity protection. Do not purchase or wire that part as a pogo backfeed barrier. Existing research on BQ24074, BQ24031 and Pololu motor paths remains applicable but not qualified.

Decision: SOURCE_REVIEWED only; neither part selected, no hardware copy. Actionable hardware blocker: select adapter voltage, protected 1S cell, charger, 5 V converter, motor power disconnect and exact external blocking FET topology; draw dock contact to charger, boost, USB and motor rail fault current paths. Then create topology specific source tests for contact bounce, reversed contact, absent adapter, failed FET, WROOM reset and stale dock evidence, and measure reverse leakage, contact energy, inrush and thermals on bench. Status PENDING hardware integration; no tests executed or physical charging qualification. Commercial licensing and exact board revision still unresolved.

### November 14 research: BQ25895 NVDC SYS is not the robot's 5 V rail

Owner: Robot Open Source Research. Reconciled current PR #17 head 144142869c960a372a74bd9262cfbc8122ab2b0e (2005 commits), 65 issue comments, 65 combined timeline entries, zero submitted reviews and zero inline threads. Reviewed existing code/scripts/power_preflight.py and tests/test_power_preflight.py; mobile and docked budgets and the no-physical-qualification flags already exist, so no duplicate sizing source change.

Upstream open-source hardware notes: brickbots/PiFinder docs/bq25895_design_notes.md https://github.com/brickbots/PiFinder/blob/release/docs/bq25895_design_notes.md documents BQ25895 NVDC power path, SYS just above cell voltage, internal RBFET/BATFET and the PMID OTG node. This is a useful topology reference, NOT a validated 5 V dock or a license to copy the whole board. Verify exact PiFinder hardware revision and repository licensing before any CAD reuse. Manufacturer: TI BQ25895 datasheet and charger documentation, https://www.ti.com/product/BQ25895 . Firmware reference: electricimp/BQ25895 https://github.com/electricimp/BQ25895 exposes I2C charge-voltage/current configuration but targets Electric Imp Squirrel, not ESP32; porting it directly would add an unnecessary runtime and its defaults (4.208 V, 2048 mA; M variant 4.352 V) MUST NOT be silently applied to an unselected cell. Decision: BQ25895 is RESEARCH_ONLY. The NVDC SYS output tracks the battery region and does not replace a verified independent regulated 5 V motor/head rail; OTG/PMID capability is not proof of safe exposed pogo contacts, reverse blocking, or USB isolation. Do not enable OTG on an exposed dock connection without an independently verified physical isolation design. No charger or cell selected.

Followup: owner Hardware Integration must choose exact protected 1S cell, charger board revision, 5 V regulator, contact protection and thermistor configuration, and document a schematic showing SYS/BAT/PMID/USB/dock and the WROOM motor disconnect.
